研 究 報 告                
中砂(1560.TT) 
產業：電機機械 
上修資本支出用於擴產隱含展望正向，看好毛利率延續成⻑趨勢 
 投資評價與建議 
3Q26毛利率表現優於預期，且公司上修資本支出用於擴產，隱含主要業務展
望正向，加上公司積極優化產品結構隱含明年營收、獲利均具上修空間；我們
給予40倍PE，維持對公司業務之正向看法，並看好毛利率維持成⻑動能。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26三大業務營收、獲利持續改善：觀察ABU業務中，預期高階砂輪與ABF鑽
⽯機密模具需求持續成⻑，DBU業務出貨動能則維持強勁，加上公司規劃針對
SBU部分品項漲價約10%，將帶動營收、毛利率改善。我們預估本季營收有望
季增5.5%至28.4億元，毛利率44.5%，稅後EPS 4.37元。 
上修今年資本支出預算用於擴產與新品開發，隱含營運前景正向：基於DBU、
SBU客戶需求強勁，及公司持續優化產品組合，公司上修今年資本支出預算至
22.6億元(原16~17億元)，除了先前已規畫SBU 3Q27月產能至43萬片(已宣
布)，本次則上修今明年底鑽⽯碟月產能分別至8、11萬顆(原7萬顆以上/9.5萬
顆)，此外將購買土地廠房用於開發先進封裝用之減薄鑽⽯砂輪新品，以及後續
鑽⽯碟擴產需求。而在2027年展望方面，我們仍維持2027年三大業務均正向
成⻑之看法，其中DBU、SBU成⻑動能最為強勁，另ABU業務中，公司布局之
先進封裝用之減薄砂輪有望於2H27貢獻營收。預估2027年營收年增25.7%至
129.7億元，毛利率46%，稅後EPS 21.7元。 
 投資風險 
DBU擴產進度不如預期。

| 營運概況 
3Q26毛利率表現優於預期：3Q26營收季增8.1%至26.9億元，毛利率42.2%，稅後EPS 3.88元，其中毛利率受惠
於：(1)台灣工具機客戶需求回溫、ABF鑽⽯機密模具用量增加，帶動ABU高階產品比重提升，挹注該業務毛利率
由2Q26的超過25%提升至3Q26的35%；(2)針對DBU大客戶之2/1.6奈米、先進封裝鑽⽯碟用量持續增加，使營收
規模擴大，我們推估毛利率由2Q26的50~55%提升至55%以上。 
4Q26三大業務營收、獲利持續改善：觀察：(1)ABU業務中，預期高階砂輪與ABF鑽⽯機密模具需求持續成⻑，至
於泰國、日本子公司出貨動能則持平看待，預估營收微幅季增，毛利率則有望持續向上；(2)DBU業務中，出貨動
能則維持強勁，因台系大客戶先進製程、封裝需求維持強勁，另美系記憶體、IDM客戶需求呈現逐季成⻑，加上
成熟製程廠商產能利用率持續提升，預估營收仍有雙位數季增；(3)SBU業務中，12吋月產能維持40萬片滿載，但
公司計畫針對部分品項價格調漲約10%，加上持續優化產品組合，將帶動營收、毛利率改善。預估本季營收有望
季增5.5%至28.4億元，毛利率44.5%，稅後EPS 4.37元。 
上修今年資本支出預算用於擴產與新品開發，隱含營運前景正向：基於DBU、SBU客戶需求強勁，及公司持續優
化產品組合，公司上修今年資本支出預算至22.6億元(原16~17億元)，除了先前已規畫SBU 3Q27月產能至43萬片
(已宣布)，本次則上修今明年底鑽⽯碟月產能分別至8、11萬顆(原7萬顆以上/9.5萬顆)，此外將購買土地廠房用於
開發先進封裝用之減薄鑽⽯砂輪新品，以及後續鑽⽯碟擴產需求。而在2027年展望方面，我們仍維持2027年三大
業務均正向成⻑之看法，動能包含：(1)預估DBU主要客戶A16/2、3奈米明年底月產能將由今年底的9~10、15~17
分別提升至14~16、19~20萬片，尤其先進製程CMP道數、規格相較於成熟製程提升，將帶動鑽⽯碟用量、平均
單價增加，另美系IDM合作順利(目前公司於18A市占約50%，公司持續推進14A之合作)，加上成熟製程、記憶體
客戶擴產挹注；(2)SBU除了有明年額外3萬片之產能挹注，公司針對現有產能也將持續透過產品組合優化等方式改
善獲利，且看好2H27美系記憶體特殊晶圓用量將比1H27成⻑；(3)ABU方面，則基於台灣ABF廠商持續擴產，公
司布局之新品先進封裝減薄砂輪有望在2H27於新廠貢獻營收，加上公司將中低毛利率產品搬至泰國、日本子公司
生產以此優化成本結構。我們預估2027年營收年增25.7%至129.7億元，毛利率46%，稅後EPS 21.7元。

## Document 084: 2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026年 11月16日

1/5 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（１次速報値） 
～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅マイナス成長も、景気回復の基調は維持 
本日内閣府から公表された 2026年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率（１次速報）は前期比年率
▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と、４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった1。個人消費が前期比▲0.3％
と減少したほか、住宅投資や公共投資も減少し、成長率を押し下げている。一方、設備投資は前
期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えした。概ね事前の市場予想（前期比年率＋0.2％、
筆者予想：同▲0.4％）の範囲内であり、サプライズはない。 
今回減少した個人消費については、過度に悲観する必要はないだろう。４-６月期にみられた自
動車やエアコンなどの耐久財需要の一時的な押し上げが一巡したことに加え、７-９月期には台風
や大雨、長雨などの天候不順が相次いだことも下押し要因となったとみられる。一方、名目賃金
の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、家計の所得環境は足元で改善している。食料品
や日用品などを中心とした物価の高止まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、今回の減
少をもって個人消費の基調が悪化したと判断する必要はないだろう。 
一方、設備投資が高い伸びとなったことは好材料だ。今回の増加には前期の特殊要因の反動も
含まれているが、それを除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持す
るなか、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。企業
部門の底堅さが改めて確認されたと評価できる。 
このように、７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰは４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となったが、減少幅は僅か
だ。前期まで３四半期連続で比較的高い成長が続いていたことに加え、今回の個人消費の減少に
は、耐久財需要の剥落や８～９月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因が影響したとみられる
点も重要だ。今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろ
う。家計部門は力強さを欠く一方、設備投資を中心に企業部門は堅調さを維持している。家計と
企業の回復に濃淡はみられるものの、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると評価で
きる。

1 ＧＤＰ統計では、季節調整のかけ直しや基礎統計の改定に伴い過去の値も毎回改定される。25年４-６月期が前期比
年率＋0.8％（改定前＋0.5％）、７-９月期が▲1.5％（同▲1.5％）、10-12月期が＋1.1％（同＋1.1％）、26年１-３月期
が＋1.9％（同＋1.9％）、４-６月期が＋1.7％（同＋1.4％）などとなった。符号の変化等はみられず、景気認識に変更
を迫るほどではなかった。

### Page 2

2/5

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
今回のＧＤＰを評価する際に注意が必要なのは、輸入と公的在庫の動きである。 
４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を通
じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油が
放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt（前
期比年率寄与度▲2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げは、
公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じた。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって原油輸入が
回復したため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因となった。一方、輸入の回復に伴って国
家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小した。この
結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.5％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大き
く押し上げている。 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げ（前期比年率寄与度▲2.2％Pt）と公的在庫変動による押し上げ
（同寄与度＋2.0％Pt）は、ともに原油の供給環境が改善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別
に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が回避されたことである。ＧＤ
Ｐ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用可能
な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むしろ前
向きに捉えるべきだろう。

10-12月期はプラス成長を予想 
 先行きについても、景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持すると予想する。７-９月期は４四半期ぶり
のマイナス成長となったが、耐久財特需の剥落や天候不順による一時的な下押しが和らぐことか
ら、10-12月期には再びプラス成長に戻る可能性が高い。名目賃金の高い伸びや雇用の改善が続く
なか、所得環境の改善が個人消費を下支えするとみられる。 
ただし、今後は物価上昇率の再加速が個人消費の重荷になると予想される。既往の原油高や原
材料費、物流費などの上昇が時間差を伴って川下へ波及しつつある。今後は食料品や日用品など
への価格転嫁がさらに進むことに加え、電気・ガス代も上昇するだろう。物価上昇率の加速によ
り、足元で続いている実質賃金の改善が一服する恐れがある。個人消費は先行き持ち直すとみる
が、回復ペースは力強さを欠くとみられる。 
一方、企業部門については今後も底堅い推移が期待できる。企業収益が高水準を維持するな
か、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。輸入資本
財価格や建設コストの上昇は下押し要因となるものの、設備投資は緩やかな増加基調を続けると
予想する。また、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さやグローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大も、輸

### Page 3

3/5 
出や生産を下支えするだろう。 
このように、10-12月期は個人消費が緩やかに持ち直すことに加え、設備投資や輸出の増加が景
気を下支えし、再びプラス成長になると予想する。物価高による個人消費への下押しには注意が
必要だが、企業部門の堅調さが維持されることで、日本経済は緩やかな回復を続けるだろう。

需要項目別の詳細 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と２四半期連続で減少した。形態別では、耐久財消費が前期比▲
3.5％（４-６月期：同＋3.6％）、非耐久財消費が同▲0.2％（４-６月期：同▲1.9％）、半耐久財
消費が同▲1.4％（４-６月期：同＋0.4％）、サービス消費が同＋0.1％（４-６月期：同▲0.1％）
となっている。４-６月期には環境性能割の廃止を受けた自動車販売の増加や、27年４月の省エネ
基準強化を控えたエアコンの前倒し購入など、耐久財消費の特需がみられたが、７-９月期にはこ
うした押し上げ効果が一巡した。また、８～９月には記録的な豪雨や台風、長雨などの天候不順
が相次いだことが、外出手控えによる消費機会の減少や旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて下押し
に繋がったとみられる。また、非耐久財消費にも弱さがみられる。食料品や日用品の価格上昇が
続くなか、家計の節約志向が根強いことに加え、天候不順による季節商品の販売減少なども下押
しに効いた可能性がある。 
もっとも、名目賃金の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、所得環境は足元で改善し
ており、家計を取り巻く環境が悪化しているわけではない。食料品を中心とした価格水準の高止
まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、４-６月期には高校授業料の実質無償化拡充や学
校給食費無償化に伴う個人消費から政府消費への振り替わりなどの特殊要因があり、今回７-９月
期についても耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが大きく影響したとみられ
る。個人消費が力強さを欠いていることは確かだが、今回の減少をもって消費の基調が悪化した
と判断する必要はないだろう。 
先行きについては、耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが和らぐことに加
え、賃金上昇や雇用の改善が下支えとなることから、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直すと予想する。
ただし、26年度後半には既往の輸入コスト上昇の価格転嫁が進み、食料品や日用品などの値上が
りや電気・ガス代の上昇が家計の実質購買力を圧迫する可能性が高い。物価上昇率の再加速によ
って実質賃金の改善が一服するリスクがあり、消費の回復ペースは当面緩やかなものにとどまる
だろう。 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、３四半期ぶりに増加した。４-６月期には特許権
の海外譲渡に伴う研究開発投資の減少という特殊要因により押し下げられていたが、７-９月期に
はその影響が剥落したことが前期比での押し上げ要因となった。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を
除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応
した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は引き続き強い。各種調査でも企業の設
備投資計画は高い伸びを維持しており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断
できる。

### Page 4

4/5
先行きについても、輸入資本財価格や建設コストの上昇が設備投資の下押し要因となるもの
の、高水準の企業収益や根強い省力化・デジタル・ＡＩ関連投資需要が下支えとなり、緩やかな
増加基調が続くと予想する。
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れている。
両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減となった。４-６月期には大口要因によって研究開発サービスの
受取が急増していたが、７-９月期にはその反動によってサービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（４-６月
期：＋8.9％）と大幅に減少した。一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（４-６月期：▲0.8％）と持ち
直している。グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、半導体製造装置などの資本財輸出が好
調だったほか、４-６月期に大きく落ち込んだ中東向け輸出に回復の動きがみられたことも財輸出

の押し上げ要因になった。サービス輸出の特殊要因による振れを除けば、海外景気の底堅さとＡ
Ｉ関連需要の拡大を背景に、輸出は緩やかな増加基調を維持していると評価できる。
先行きについても、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さに加え、グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の
拡大が半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出を下支えすることから、緩やかな増加基調が続くと
予想する。
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と増加した。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が減少していた
が、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％Pt（前期比年率寄与度▲2.4％Pt）と大幅なマイナスになった。
ただし、輸入増加による成長率の押し下げは、原油などの供給環境が改善したことに伴うもの
であり、需要の弱さを示すものではない。また、輸入増加の裏側では、国家備蓄原油の取り崩し
が一服したことに伴って公的在庫変動が前期比で＋0.5％ポイント、前期比年率では＋2.0％ポイ
ントの大幅プラス寄与となっている。両者は一体として評価する必要があるだろう。

民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.0％Ptと、成長率にはほぼニュートラルだった。製品在庫と原
材料在庫が押し上げに寄与した一方、流通在庫と仕掛品在庫が下押し要因になった。
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と、２四半期連続で減少した。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因となっ
ており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。先行きについても、建設コストの高止まり
や金利上昇の影響から、低調な推移が続く可能性が高い。
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と概ね横ばいとなった。４-６月期に前期比＋1.5％と高い伸びとなっ
た後、増勢が一服している。もっとも、医療・介護関連の支出などは趨勢的に増加しており、政
府消費の弱さは前期の反動による一時的なものと考えられる。
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と減少した。政府の国土強靱化関連工事などが下支えにはなっている
が、建設コストの上昇が実質公共投資の下押し要因になっているとみられる。

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月消費統計 
サービスを中心に消費全体が弱く、前月から減少

経済調査部 エコノミスト 龐 鈞文

[要約] 
◼ 2026年9月の家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（CTIミクロ）
で見ても実質消費は同▲1.6％と 2カ月連続で減少した。また、供給側統計の 1つであ
る商業動態統計では、CPI の財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じ
て見れば、9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。 
◼ 先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金は最低賃金の引き上げもあ
って上昇が続く見込みである。一方、政府の物価高対策が予定されるものの、原油高
などによる幅広い分野への価格上昇の波及、円安や人件費上昇による価格転嫁などを
通じて個人消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。

＜2026年9月の消費総括＞サービスを中心に消費全体が弱い 
需要側統計である家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比▲2.8％と3カ月
ぶりに減少した。複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（CTI ミクロ）で見て
も実質消費は同▲1.6％と 2カ月連続で減少した（図表 1）。 また、供給側統計の 1つである商
業動態統計では、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。総じて見れば、
2026年 9月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。

＜CTIミクロ・家計調査（需要側）＞全ての費目が減少 
2026年 9月の CTIミクロ（二人以上の世帯）を費目別に見ると、10大費目1のうち、「教養娯
楽」（前月比▲3.3％）、「教育」（同▲6.5％）、「食料」（同▲0.9％）、「光熱・水道」（同▲
3.1％）、「家具・家事用品」（同▲4.8％）、「交通・通信」（同▲0.8％）、「保健医療」（同
▲2.4％）、「住居」（同▲1.6％）、「被服及び履物」（同▲3.0％）、「その他」（同▲0.4％）の全
ての費目が減少した（図表 2）。全ての費目が減少したのは消費税率が 10％に引き上げられた
2019年 10月以来だ。

### Page 3

1

3 /9

続いて、CTIミクロの10大費目に含まれる個別品目への支出について確認する。「教養娯楽」
は2カ月連続で減少した。教養娯楽用品や教養娯楽耐久財を中心に支出が減少した。「教育」は
3カ月連続で減少した。授業料等を中心に支出が減少した2。「食料」は3カ月ぶりに減少した。
飲料を中心に、穀類などへの支出が減少した。「光熱・水道」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。電気代
を中心に、上下水道料などへの支出が減少した。「家具・家事用品」は2カ月連続で減少した。
家庭用耐久財を中心に、家事雑貨などへの支出が減少した。「交通・通信」は2カ月連続で減少
した。自動車等関係費や通信を中心に支出が減少した。「保健医療」は3カ月連続で減少した。
保健医療用品・器具を中心に支出が減少した。「住居」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。設備修繕・維
持を中心に支出が減少した。「被服及び履物」は2カ月連続で減少した。シャツ・セーター類や
他の被服を中心に支出が減少した。「その他」は2カ月連続で減少した。諸雑費を中心に支出が
減少した。 
消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると（大和総研による季節調整値、図表3中段右）、サービス
（前月比▲3.9％）が2カ月連続で減少し、全体を押し下げた。非耐久財（同▲1.6％）は2カ月
ぶりに、耐久財（同▲3.6％）は 5カ月連続で、半耐久財（同▲3.0％）は 2カ月連続で、いず
れも減少した。 
家計調査における基礎的支出は前月比▲0.6％と2カ月ぶりに減少した。また、選択的支出は
同▲7.6％と 3カ月ぶりに大幅に減少した（いずれも大和総研による季節調整値、図表 3中段
左）。

2 ただし、授業料等は子どもや学生がいる一部の世帯以外はほとんど支出しないため、サンプルサイズが小さ
く振れが大きい。

＜商業動態統計（供給側）＞小売販売額は名目・実質ともに 2カ月連続で減少 
2026年 9月の商業動態統計によると、名目小売販売額は前月比▲0.7％と 2カ月連続で減少
した（図表 4、5）。また、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.0％だった。 
名目小売販売額を業種別に見ると、7業種中 4 業種が前月から減少した。「その他小売業」
（前月比▲2.1％）、「燃料小売業」（同▲2.7％）ともに 2カ月連続で減少した。「機械器具小売
業」（同▲2.3％）も 2カ月連続で減少した。家電大型専門店の販売額（大和総研による季節調
整値）（同▲3.2％）などが減少した。「飲食料品小売業」（同▲0.2％）は 2カ月連続で減少し
た。コンビニのファーストフード及び日配食品（同▲2.3％）と加工食品（同▲1.9％）が減少
した。 
他方、3業種が前月から増加した。「自動車小売業」（前月比＋0.1％）は 5カ月ぶりにプラス
に転じた。9月の新車販売台数（大和総研による季節調整値）は同＋2.0％と増加しており3、
この結果とも整合的だ。「織物・衣服・身の回り品小売業」（同＋4.2％）は2カ月ぶりに増加し
た。気温が低下したこと（巻末図表「全国の平均気温・日照時間・降水量」）や休日数が多か
ったことなどにより、秋物商品の販売が好調だったとみられる。「各種商品小売業」（同＋
2.1％）は 2カ月ぶりに増加した。スーパーの商品販売額（同＋1.0％）と百貨店の商品販売額
（同＋0.5％）が増加した。

7 /9

＜2026年10月の消費＞9月から減少を見込む 
業界統計や個社データ、JCB消費 NOWをもとに判断すると、2026年10月の消費は9月から減
少したとみている4。財消費では、10月前半の実績をもとに試算した家電のJCB消費額（大和総
研による季節調整値）、コンビニの JCB消費額（同）、新車販売台数（同）とアパレルの売上高
は減少した。一方、百貨店の売上高は堅調に推移した。サービス消費では、宿泊の JCB消費額
（同）は前月から減少した一方、外食のJCB消費額（同）は増加した。

＜先行き＞個人消費は底堅く推移する見込み／価格上昇の広がりに注意 
先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金の高い伸びが下支えとなるだろう。
ただし、原油高の波及や円安などによる物価上昇が実質賃金を押し下げ、消費の重荷となる可
能性には注意が必要だ。 
名目賃金の伸びは高水準で推移するだろう。厚生労働省によると、全ての都道府県で 10～12
月に最低賃金が引き上げられる。2026年度の最低賃金の全国加重平均額は1,177円で5、引き上
げ率は＋5.0％（前年度は＋6.3％）と高水準を維持した。また、企業収益の改善などを背景に、
高水準の年末賞与が期待される。 
消費者物価について、12月使用分から 2027年 2月使用分までの電気・ガス料金の補助とい
った政府の物価高対策が実施される予定だ6。ただし、原油高などにより、原材料費や輸送費の
上昇を通じて幅広い分野の価格に波及し、円安や人件費の高騰などによる価格転嫁も物価を押
し上げ、消費を下押しする可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。また、帝国データバンクによる
と、飲食料品の 9～10月の大規模な値上げラッシュは一服したが、11月の値上げ品目数は前年
11月の約 10倍規模となり、引き続き高水準で推移している。賃上げや物流費、エネルギーコ
ストの上昇など、食品を取り巻くコスト環境が楽観視できる状況にはないため、値上げは続く
とみている7。 
また、金利上昇による住宅ローン等の利払い負担増が個人消費にもたらす悪影響にも警戒す
る必要がある8。さらに、消費動向調査（内閣府）によると、10 月の消費者態度指数（二人以
上の世帯、季節調整値）は 35.0（前月差▲0.4pt）と 2カ月連続で小幅に低下し、依然として
低水準にあり（巻末図表「消費者マインド」参照）、節約志向の高まりに引き続き注意が必要
だ。

4 詳細は、拙稿「消費データブック（2026/11/5号）」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 5日）を参照。 
5 厚生労働省「全ての都道府県で地域別最低賃金の改定額が答申されました ～答申での全国加重平均額は昨
年度から 56円引上げの 1,177円～」（2026年9月3日） 
6 資源エネルギー庁「電気・ガス料金支援」（2026年11月9日閲覧） 
7 帝国データバンク「『食品主要 195社』価格改定動向調査 ― 2026年 11月 飲食料品値上げ、年間 2万品
目超え 11月は 1477品目 秋の『値上げラッシュ』は一服」（2026年10月 30日） 
8 詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026年8月」（大和総研レポート、 
2026年8月24日）を参照。

上銀(2049) 
買進, TP 450 (潛在報酬 54.37%)  
買進 
Price(11/10)：291.5 
Target Price：450

26Q3 受惠終端需求強勁，營收達 81.10 億元，QoQ 8.01%，YoY 
35.27%；受惠稼動率提升與價格策略帶動毛利走高，單季 EPS 達
3.48 元。10 月營收延續強勁動能，達 28.50 億元，MoM 4.79%，
YoY 35.66%，累計前 10 月營收為 248.48 億元，YoY 25.11%。
目前整體市場供不應求，訂單能見度已直達 2027 年中。 
展望後市，隨公司下半年增聘逾 300 名員工並持續加班生產，26
年底產出量將較年初成長 25%至 30%，預期 26Q4 將打破歐美長
假與盤點淡季效應，營運有望優於 26Q3。研究部預估 26Q4 營收
為 85.10 億元，QoQ 4.94%，YoY 30.86%；毛利率估為 35.11%，
營業利益 12.53 億元，QoQ -13.32%，YoY 297.33%；單季 EPS
為 2.95 元。綜合考量下游客戶需求持續暢旺、公司優先承接高毛
利訂單使獲利結構性優化，並已長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機，研
究部預估 26 與 27 年營收為 305.08 億元與 344.40 億元，YoY 
25.74%與 12.89%；毛利率為 34.54%與 35.11%；營業利益為 43.63
億元與 58.60 億元，YoY 165.52%與 34.30%；稅後獲利為 36.93
億元與 48.07 億元，YoY 142.05%與 30.15%；預估 26 與 27 年
EPS 分別為 10.44 元與 13.59 元。 
評價方面，上銀過去五年本益比主要集中於 20 倍至 40 倍區間。
考量公司整體營收與獲利將呈現雙位數之成長上升趨勢，且自動化
與傳動產業結構持續復甦上行，研究部以 2027 年預估 EPS 13.59
元作為評價基礎，並給予歷史區間中緣偏上之 33 倍 PER，目標價
設定為 450 元，給予買進之投資建議。     
⚫   ⚫ 公司基本資料 
上銀科技(2049)成立於 1989 年，主要從事線性滑軌(GW)、滾
螺桿(BS)、工業機器人、機器手臂、迴轉工作台、減速機、精
密軸承及醫療設備之研究開發、設計、製造、銷售、維修及售
前售後服務，同時為全球第三大滾珠螺桿廠和線性滑軌廠，僅
次於日本 THK、NSK。公司以自有品牌「HIWIN」行銷全球，
海外子公司橫跨德國、美國、日本、瑞士、捷克、法國，

⚫ 26Q3 EPS 為 3.48 元，機器人營收年成長 8 成 
營收 81.10 億，QoQ 8.01%，YoY 35.27%，除歐洲市場外，
其餘市場皆呈現年紀雙成長，其中機器人相關產品年增 82%。 
獲利表現方面，毛利率 36.34%，季增 2.18 個百分點，年增 
7.74 個百分點，提升主要因(1)漲價效益浮現、(2)UTR 提升、

⚫ 26Q4 有望淡季不淡，估營收季增 4.94%達 85.10 億 
上銀 10 月營收 28.50，YoY 35.66%，MoM4.79%，累計
營收為 248.48 億， YoY25.11%。目前下游需求仍強勁，政
體市場處供不應求之態勢，隨 26H2 增聘逾 300 名員工並採
加班生產，26 年底產出量較年初成長 25%～30%，預期 
26Q4 有望突破歐美長假與盤點淡季效應，營運有望優於 
26Q3。 
研究部預估，26Q4 營收 85.10 億，QoQ 4.94%，YoY 30.86%；
毛利率 35.11%，季減 1.23 個百分點，年增 8.07 個百分點；
營業利益 12.53 億，QoQ -13.32%，YoY 297.33%；稅後獲
利 10.45 億，QoQ -14.42%，YoY 126.22%；EPS 為 2.95 元。

⚫ 訂單滿載，能見度達 2027 年中 
估計 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 10.44/13.59 元。 
目前下游需求仍呈現供不應求，研磨級滾珠螺桿交期已拉長至 
6.5 個月以上，線性滑軌依產品規格亦維持 4～6 個月，整體
訂單能見度直達 2027 年中，且 26Q3 合約負債達 1.34 億
元（YoY +126%）。 
各區域表現方面，亞洲市場受惠中國大陸工具機產業升級，帶
動傳動元件需求持續成長，同時台灣與亞洲各國受惠半導體製
造需求熱絡，推升機器人、馬達與旋轉轉台等零組件出貨；美
國受惠製造業回流、半導體擴產與 AI 資料中心建置潮，帶動
強勁設備需求；歐洲雖受車市疲軟影響而整體持平，但軍工與
潛在戰後重建需求已逐步浮現。 
獲利與產能方面，管理層表示現階段不傾向全面漲價，而採取
融資率 4.25 -0.09 
券餘(張) 57 23

⚫ 長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機 
機器人業務佔 26Q3 營收達 12%，其中 EFEM 隨半導體需
求熱絡穩健成長，並已超前部署對應 FOPLP 方片規格之移載
系統。公司充分發揮垂直整合優勢，自製減速機、螺桿、軸承，
並整合關係企業大銀微系統之伺服馬達與驅動器。目前已獲美
系人形機器人廠商小批量線性滑軌訂單，美系 AI 物流機器人
放量時程則取決於客戶端實測進度。CPO 方面，大銀微已開
發出奈米級定位平台，並整合上銀微小型螺桿與線軌，共同搶
攻次世代半導體設備商機。

⚫ 結論與建議: 
26Q3 受惠終端需求強勁，營收達 81.10 億元，QoQ 8.01%，
YoY 35.27%；受惠稼動率提升與價格策略帶動毛利走高，單
季 EPS 達 3.48 元。10 月營收延續強勁動能，達 28.50 億元，
MoM 4.79%，YoY 35.66%，累計前 10 月營收為 248.48 億元，
YoY 25.11%。目前整體市場供不應求，訂單能見度已直達
2027 年中。    
展望後市，隨公司下半年增聘逾 300 名員工並持續加班生產，
26 年底產出量將較年初成長 25%至 30%，預期 26Q4 將打破
歐美長假與盤點淡季效應，營運有望優於 26Q3。研究部預估
26Q4 營收為 85.10 億元，QoQ 4.94%，YoY 30.86%；毛利
率估為 35.11%，營業利益 12.53 億元，QoQ -13.32%，YoY 
297.33%；單季 EPS 為 2.95 元。    
綜合考量下游客戶需求持續暢旺、公司優先承接高毛利訂單使
獲利結構性優化，並已長期佈局機器人與 CPO 商機，研究部

### Page 1

Simplo Technology (6121.TWO)
An Established Battery Franchise with Room for a Better Mix
CITI'S TAKE 
3Q26 EPS missed our/BBG estimates by 35%/30% each as notebook pull-
ins faded and low yields on a major US customer’s new smartphone held 
back shipments. We expect higher PC component costs to keep IT demand 
subdued in 1Q27E, while rising contribution from AES could still help margin 
expansion in 2027E. We lower 2026-2028E EPS by 17-20% and revise our 
TP to NT$400 based on 12x 2027E EPS, lowered from 13x reflecting weaker 
visibility on IT recovery. We continue to favor Simplo’s leading market share 
in notebook/smartphone battery franchises and its exposure to AIDC BBU 
growth through AES. 
3Q earnings -17% y/y missed our/BBG estimates by 35%/30% on weaker IT 
shipments — 3Q26 sales of NT$19.9bn fell 2% y/y and 5% q/q, 13%/10% below 
our/BBG forecasts. Notebook customers pulled orders into 1H26 amid rising 
memory prices, while lower yields on a major US customer’s new smartphone 
constrained 3Q shipment. Group GPM rose 0.7ppt y/y to 16.3% on a larger AES 
contribution. However, standalone GPM dropped 1.6ppt y/y 50 9.2% on higher input 
costs and lower volumes. Group OPM rose 0.4ppt y/y to 9.7%, lower than our (12.2%) 
and BBG (11.9%) estimates. Higher taxes also weighed on EPS. 
2027E outlook stays muted for IT — The mgmt. expects IT demand to remain 
lukewarm in 1Q27E as higher component costs put upward pressure on PC prices 
likely weighing on demand. Standalone sales are expected to be flat to slightly down 
y/y in 2027E, with the group sales flat to slightly up on AES’s BBU growth. Mgmt. 
notes the battery-pack inventories at notebook brands are lean at just a few weeks 
and easing cobalt prices could bring down ASP in 2027E. A new non-IT product line 
is growing severalfold from a small base, and the mgmt. expects it to make up >5% 
in 2028E with a GPM above group’s average. 
Implications — We cut 2026-2028E earnings estimates 17-20% for 2026-2028E 
reflecting 3Q miss and a slower IT recovery, resulting in a 10-18% cut on our sales 
forecasts. We lower our TP to NT$400 based on a target P/E of 12x from 13x on 2027E 
EPS, reflecting the component inflation likely delaying the timing of IT recovery. We 
stay Buy on Simplo’s established notebook and smartphone battery franchises and 
AES’s exposure to growing datacenter BBU demand. AI PC adoption could lift 
battery-pack ASPs over time, and an estimated 6% dividend yield should help 
cushion downside.

Simplo Technology (6121.TWO)

Simplo Technology
Company description
Established in 1992, Simplo specializes in designing and producing battery
packs for Notebooks, tablet PC, and electronic vehicle products. With strong
execution and cost advantages, Simplo continues to broaden its client base.
Its major customers include PC OEMs (such as HPQ, Dell, Asus, Acer, and
Apple) and NB ODMs (such as Quanta, Compal, Wistron, and Inventec).

Investment strategy
We rate Simplo shares as Buy. Simplo’s heating fundamentals are driven by
the following reasons. 1) Japan supplier's strategy change on focusing EV
battery pack and de-prioritizing NB which provides Simplo more orders wins;
and 2)  Non-IT: according to our industry survey, Simplo has been qualified by
global ISPs for the battery back units (BBU) for their data centers.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$400 is based on a target P/E of 12x 2027E EPS, on par

with its historical average since 2010.
Risks
Downside risks that may cause the shares to continue to trade below our
target price include: 1) weaker than expected end product sales; 2) intensified
competition; and 3) worse-than-expected overall NB/tablet/PC growth.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +14% MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$74,666mn (+14% MoM/+30% YoY), driven by
the increasing shipment of power supply units, HVDC power racks, and liquid
cooling products.
• QTD revenue reached 35% of MSe at NT$215,881mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY),
and 34% of consensus at NT$219,806mn (+12% QoQ/+36% YoY).

Our view:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We think the slightly better Oct monthly sales should help on near-term
sentiment, though more meaningful upside to market expectations on
earnings growth might  depend on the penetration of HVDC power racks into
the coming years.
• We continue to see Delta as a key beneficiary of the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters through its comprehensive power/cooling

offerings. Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 128: 261110_gs_kinsus

Source PDF: all report pdf/261110_gs_kinsus.pdf

### Page 1

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

We hosted Kinsus’ 3Q26 post-result follow-up call today (Nov 
10th). Key takeaways include: 
(1) Kinsus mentioned it is getting more market share from existing 
server CPU customers, AI GPU customer, and ASIC customers. 
Despite not having much market share gain opportunity in its AI 
server CPU products, the company mentioned the volume growth 
could still be 150% YoY in AI server CPU shipment in 2027 vs. 2026, 
suggesting a good revenue/GM growth outlook going into 2027/28.  
(2) Kinsus guides 2027/28 revenue to go up by at least 30% YoY 
each year while GM should be at least 35/40% in 2027/28, 
considering the orders/capacity/UTR/product mix differences, 
despite the key upside risk will be on like for like base pricing hike 
(the guidance above does not include any pricing hike assumption, 
but this could lift the revenue growth by another 15ppt in both 
years although not guaranteed, per the company). 
(3) For 4Q26, the company guided revenue to go up by 10% QoQ, 
with GM being expanded into 35% (from ~34% in 3Q26), despite a 
much more favorable than 3Q product mix and ~5-10% pricing hike 
on ABF & BT substrates. For 1Q27, the company expects some 
seasonal issues on consumer electronics and contact lens, and 
believes revenue should be flat to slightly up QoQ, despite possible 
GM slowdown during the low season. 
(4) In terms of the future investment plan, Kinsus mentioned it will 
only need to invest in the land/infrastructure/construction etc. while 
end customers will take care of the equipment; normally, equipment 
accounted for 60% of the total CAPEX while the rest only accounts 
for 40% of the total investment.  
(5) Kinsus mentioned it now holds ~70% of the AI server CPU 
substrate market share, and the company believes its market share 
should be pretty stable in this product cycle. For AI GPU, Kinsus

GM to further expand in 2027/28 with a better high-end ABF exposure/market share, 
without seeing any order cut; Buy

mentioned it expects its share to increase from 10% now to 30% in 2027 for its existing
customer’s supply chain. Kinsus also mentioned it is working on several ASIC customers’
substrates product, which could also be the key revenue/profit driver for 2027 and
beyond.
(6) In terms of the order forecast, the company mentioned it has not yet seen any order
cut or order delay on its ABF substrates business, but the company continues to
maintain its cautious but positive view on the overall substrate industry outlook.

Investment view
We are positive on Kinsus’ 3Q result (GM expanded by 7.8ppt QoQ in 3Q - see here), and
believe the company’s GM expansion trend will continue in coming quarters, despite the
company only guiding 1ppt QoQ growth on GM in 4Q (however, the company is also
giving a conservative guidance on profitability. Kinsus only guided GM to be 27-28% in
3Q26, but it ended up with 33.9% in its 3Q result). Moreover, we believe Kinsus’
proactive capacity expansion plan (1 new plant every year from 2025 to 2029) will start

to help the company to outperform peers from 2Q27 (the new K6-B plant starts
contributing revenue from 2Q27) which should also help the company to enjoy better
profitability in coming quarters, as the new plants will focus on high-end projects, whose
GM is generally 10ppt+ higher than the company’s average level).
Overall, we maintain our Buy rating on Kinsus, and we expect the better market share in
AI server CPU and its better position in the high-end ABF substrate industry to

strengthen into the long term.
We maintain our Buy rating with an unchanged 12m TP of NT$1,515, based on the
same 19.5x 2028E EPS, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average
P/E (14.2x).
Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate

shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end
ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe

Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by a better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the
unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on
19.5x 2028E P/E, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average P/E

### Page 1

M
Update
Compal Electronics  | Asia Pacific
Server Ramp Takes Shape but
Valuation Keeps Us UW
Compal’s server expansion is progressing, with revenue
contribution rising to ~12% in 3Q and management targeting 30-
40% in CY27. However, weaker PC volumes and diminishing
scale benefits continue to pressure margins. At ~14x CY27E P/E,
valuation remains stretched.
3Q OpM missed. See our  3Q26 Results Summary                   .
PC business facing demand and profitability headwinds: Compal expects a mid-

teens q/q decline in 4Q26 shipments because of higher selling prices. This weakness
should persist into CY27; the company projects a likely double-digit y/y unit decline,
though it admitted that visibility remains low. However, rising ASPs from mix
changes and cost pass-through could keep 4Q PC revenue up sequentially.
Servers have become a key growth driver: Servers rose from ~5% of the mix in 1Q
to ~12% in 3Q, with a CY27 revenue contribution target of 30-40%. While 4Q26

server revenue is guided to be flat q/q, Compal is aggressively scaling its capacity. It
now primarily ships from Taiwan, and a major L10/11 ramp will begin in 2Q27 at its
new Texas plant. It currently does L10 HGX for Neocloud customers and L6 for a US
enterprise customer; it will do L10/11 for enterprise customer(s) as well, and is
actively approaching CSP customers for ASIC L11 solutions as well.
Margins to remain pressured: Overall margins were pressured by the PC segment,

driving a sequential decline in 3Q owing to lower PC volumes and utilization. This
margin headwind should intensify in 4Q because of shrinking PC business scale.
Conversely, server gross margin should remain resilient in the high single digits to
10%,  staying flat q/q.
4Q preview: We estimate that 4Q revenue will grow ~8% q/q, to NT$263bn (+38%
y/y), primarily driven by higher PC ASPs despite declining volumes. GM should
contracting ~10bps q/q, to 4.3% (-150bps y/y), with declining PC profitability, mainly

owing to diminishing scale benefits. This leads to our OP estimate of NT$2.8bn
(+25% q/q, +6% y/y) and NP estimate of NT$1.7bn (-34% q/q, +21% y/y), for EPS of
NT$0.39. Our 4Q net profit estimate is down q/q because of lower non-op income.
Stay UW with price target unchanged at NT$23.0 (10.4x CY27 P/E). We think
valuation looks quite stretched now at ~14x CY27e P/E. We prefer other ODMs like
Wistron (6x), Hon Hai (12x), Wiwynn (8x), or Quanta (9x).

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.
Request your ballot                          here.

3Q26 Results Summary
3Q26 gross margin miss:
• Revenue was NT$243bn (+2% q/q, +30% y/y), and GM was 4.4% (-30bps q/q, -
130bps y/y), 80bps and 40bps below our estimate and the Street, respectively,
driven by lower PC margins.
• As a result, OP was NT$2.bn (-21% q/q, -16% y/y), 33% below our estimate/34%
be;low the Street, for OpM of 0.9% (-30bps q/q, -50bps y/y).
• Non-op profit was higher than expected, so net profit reached Nt$2.6bn (-18% q/q,

+33% y/y), 15% above our estimate/2% above the Street, for EPS of NT$0.59.

### Page 4

Update
4
Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target is unchanged at  NT$23.0: Our price target is our base case
scenario value, derived from a multistage residual income (RI) model. We use the
company's beginning equity plus the present value of all expected residual income in
excess of the cost of capital. The RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of capital and
negative when it is below.
We assume a cost of equity of 9.1%  (beta of 1.3, equity risk premium of 6.0% and risk-free

rate of 1.5%), a medium-term growth rate of 5%, and a terminal growth rate of 3% (all
unchanged).
Our new price target implies a 2026e P/E of 10.4x and a 2027e P/E of 8.5x, below the five-
year average of 13.8x. We view these as fair, because we factor in more conservative
assumptions for its consumer business and a weaker margin outlook for Compal. The
company's venture into the "new businesses" (e.g., auto, server, etc.) is constraining its
margin profile as it tries to shift from 80%+ PC to more commercial exposure.

Our bull and bear case values are also unchanged, at NT$38.0 and NT$17.0,
respectively.

10
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
Base case scenario value, derived from a residual income valuation model. Our key assump-
tions include a cost of equity of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 13%, and a terminal
growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected iPhone sell-through
n Faster progress in AI server business
n Faster EV business development progress
n Any new M&A activity that could improve sentiment

Risks to Downside
n Lower iPhone sell-through
n Smaller profit contribution from AI business
n Slower EV business development progress

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

4576 大銀微(HMC)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研 究 員：洪 國 峰 (Titan Horng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
 在半導體先進封裝、AI 及矽光子等高階應用需求暢旺，其中
微米與奈米級定位系統交期 5~6 個月，精密運動及控制元件交
期約 3~4 個月，呈現供不應求。 
 受惠於晶圓大廠持續擴充先進製程、後段設備廠訂單持續成
長，大銀微在超高精度平台訂單已經看到 2027 年年中，另外
搭配新產能將於 2Q27 投產。預估 2027 年微米與奈米級定位
系統營收金額 31.5 億元，YoY+28.0%。 
一. 公告 3Q26 稅後 EPS 2.14 元 
大銀微 3Q26 營收金額 12.18 億元，YoY+76.73%，QoQ+19.01%，
營業利益 2.96 億元，YoY+312.6%，QoQ+61.12%，稅後盈餘 2.57
億元，YoY+255.8%，QoQ+123.72%，稅後 EPS 2.14 元。 
二. 預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.44 元 
大銀微 10/2026 營收金額 4.10 億元，YoY+68.75%，MoM-3.96%。
在訂單呈現滿載水準，預估大銀微 4Q26 營收金額 12.84 億元，
YoY+69.94% ， QoQ+5.37% ，預估營業利益 3.32 億元，
YoY+232.13% ， QoQ+12.21% ，預估稅後盈餘 2.92 億元，
YoY+223.25%，QoQ+13.78%，預估稅後 EPS 2.44 元。 
三. 預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 6.50 元 
大銀微 2026 年在半導體、PCB 與矽光子檢測裝置需求延續，帶
動微米奈米級高精度定位系統訂單維持數個月的能見度。另外中
國大陸工具機谷底復甦，推升控制元件產品需求，帶動大銀微 2026
年營運成長。預估大銀微 2026 年營收 43.8 億元，YoY+61.37%，
預估稅後盈餘 7.79 億元，YoY+222.63%，預估稅後 EPS 6.50 元。 
四. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 9.00 元 
大銀微 2027 年在推動的 One HIWIN Solution 單一零組件供應
商，往次系統、完整定位平台以及共同開發解決方案的角色移動，
搭配同集團上銀資源整合，將有助於大銀微產品附加價值提升。
宏遠預估大銀微 2027 年營收金額 53.00 億元，YoY+21.00%，預
估稅後盈餘 10.79 億元，YoY+38.52%，預估稅後 EPS 9.00 元。 
五. 投資建議買進，目標價 252 元 
觀察國內傳動元件同業 2027 年營收成長率亞德客 ROE 21%，營
收成長率 10%，PER 15X ；上銀 ROE 11% ，營收成長率 16%，
PER 22X，大銀微 ROE 21%，營收成長率 21%，PER 21.6X，以
ROE 與營收成長率相較同業來的佳，評價與同業相較仍在低檔水
準，故投資建議買進，目標價 252 元（28X 2027 年稅後 EPS）

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
大銀微系統股份有限公司創立於 1997 年 4 月，資本額 11.98 億
元，並於 2019 年 9 月正式掛牌上市。大銀微行屬於上銀集團旗
下的核心關係企業。大銀微聚焦精準定位與高性能驅動控制技
術，並以機電整合、產品製造及技術服務之整體能力作為核心競
爭優勢，深耕半導體、電子生產設備、智慧自動化及工具機等領
域。受惠半導體先進製程與封裝需求成長，大銀微以領先業界之
精密定位技術及系統整合方案擴大服務範圍，針對客戶需求提供
專業精密定位系統整合方案，透過技術互補與研發合作，協助全 
球頂尖半導體客戶縮短開發週期並提升產出效益。

二. 2026 年營運概況及 2027 年展望 
大銀行營收主要有精密運動及控制元件、微米與奈米級定位系統
與其他三部分。

預估 2027 年精密運動及控制元件營收金額 21.00 億元，
YoY+11.7%

精密運動及控制元件產品線包括：線性馬達、直驅馬達、真空馬
達、力矩馬達、伺服馬達、高速主軸馬達、位置量測系統等，並
搭配自行開發的驅動器及控制器，達奈米級高精度定位控制功
能。廣泛應用於半導體設備/自動化搬送/FPD 設備/PCB 設備
/AOI 檢測設備/SMT 設備/雷射加工器/各式機器手臂/工具機/電
動車相關/太陽能等不同產業。精密運動及控制元件 2025 年營收
金額 12.64 億元，YoY+3.1%。

精密運動及控制元件 1Q~3Q26 營收金額 13.83 億元，
YoY+45.43%。在中國大陸對驅動器需求高，目前處於供不應求狀
態，帶動包括線性馬達、直驅馬達、伺服馬達產能稼動率在高檔
水準，預估 2026 年精密運動及控制元件產品營收金額 18.80 億
元，YoY+48.7%。

預期 2027 年，在中國大陸工具機出口額與金屬切削設備產業仍呈
現正向發展，整體需求大於供給，訂單能見度己達 3~4 個月，預
估 2027 年精密運動及控制元件產品營收金額 21.00 億元，
YoY+11.7%。

微米與奈米級定位系統產品線包括：各式高精密定位平台，如單
軸、X-Y 雙軸、橋式、龍門、空氣軸承、多維度定位平台，可滿

年營收金額 14.27 億元，YoY+41.8%。

YoY+69.5%。在 AI 產業熱潮帶動半導體產業投資本支出持續加
速，間接推升微米與奈米級定位系統需求，其中半導體定位平台
能見度約 5~6 個月，超高精度平台甚至可看到 2027 年中，在此
熱潮帶動下，預估 2026 年微米與奈米級定位系統營收金額 24.60
億元，YoY+72.4%。

單持續成長，大銀微在超高精度平台訂單已經看到 2027 年年中，
包括 CoWoP、CoPoS、FoPLP 都持續配合客戶打樣中，另外搭
配新產能將於 2Q27 投 產。預 估 2027 年微米與奈米級定位系統營

大銀設立於 1997 年 4 月，為上銀的關係企
業，產品線分為精密運動及控制元件、微米
與奈米級定位系統兩大類。元件類主要產品
有線性馬達、 力矩馬達、控制器、致動器
等。定位平台為模組化產品，為客製化產品。
公司與上銀共同以「HIWIN」自有品牌行銷
全球，服務地區涵蓋美國、德國、日本、瑞
士、捷克、以色列等。相關同業如台達、匯
川等。

林其美 iris.lin@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

大銀微系統 (4576 TT) 
短中期成長動能明確

永豐觀點 
定位平台在手訂單 5-6 個月，元件訂單能見度 3 個月、交期 2-2.5 個月，短中期
成長動能明確。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進建議：公司最大應用領域為半導體行業，定位平台在手訂單 5-6 個月，元
件訂單能見度 3 個月、交期 2-2.5 個月，半導體應用成長顯著、矽光子為下一波成
長動能，中長期公司將提升元件比重、進入 stage 新應用領域。公司短、中期成長
動能明確，維持買進建議，目標價為 224 元（31 X 2027EPS）。

### Page 2

2

大銀微簡介：大銀設立於 1997 年 4 月，為上銀的關係企業，產品線分為精密運動
及控制元件、微米與奈米級定位系統兩大類。元件類主要產品有線性馬達、 力矩
馬達、控制器、致動器等。定位平台為模組化產品，為客製化產品。公司與上銀共
同以「HIWIN」自有品牌行銷全球，服務地區涵蓋美國、德國、日本、瑞士、捷克、
以色列等。相關同業如台達、匯川等。 
3Q26 EPS 達 2.14 元，達新高水準：3Q26 營收 8.55 億元（+45.5%YoY），毛利
率達 42.4%，稅後淨利 2.57 億元（+257%YoY）， EPS 為 2.14 元，創下歷史新高；
累計前三季，EPS 為 4.06 元。第三季毛利率上升乃因 stage 出貨佔比增加以及規
模經濟發揮，本季 stage 佔比上升至 58%。 
訂單能見度 2-6 個月，產能持續擴充當中：現階段客戶訂單與出貨進度進展未有
變化，stage 訂單能見度 5-6 個月、交期 3 個月，元件訂單能見度 3 個月、交期
2-2.5 個月，stage 產能擴充第一階段已完成，反映在八月營收突破四億元，第二
階段預計明年 2-3 月完成，4 月可開始貢獻營收，目前已開始規畫第三階段擴廠；
元件明年投入自動化、半自動化機台來提升產能。公司預計今年整體資本支出 1.2-
1.5 億元。 
提升元件比重、stage 進入新應用領域為未來發展方向：現階段元件比重達 40%，
公司長期目標為 45%-50%，大銀微產品搭配上銀傳動元件來擴展市場，元件產品
潛在市場規模是 stage 的十倍，加以可以進行自動化或半自動化量產，規模擴大後
規模經濟效益更明顯。在 stage 產品，公司產品目前應用在非接觸式製程，未來將
進入精密鑽孔、塔式分選、Hybrid Bonding 等接觸式製程新領域；矽光子領域產
品已於 8 月開始出貨，產品應用在 insertion 3 光學引擎測試以及系統及測試，訂
單能見度很高，已可看到 2027 年中，使用公司產品可解決巡光耗時以及微小震動
可能導致光損，此可提升客戶光學檢測效率，與每小時產出量。 
毛利率預計可維持在 40%：在毛利率方面，公司預計第四季可維持在 40%，中長
期目標也在 40%上下，明年新產能開出與新產品、新領域的開拓，加上公司採用
自動化與半自動化提升效率、優化成本結構，毛利率可維持在 40%。 
財務預估： 2026 年 營 收 43.12 億元(+58.8 ％ YoY) ， 稅 後 淨 利 7.16 億元
（+196%YoY），EPS 為 5.98 元。2027 年營收 51.02 億元（+18%YoY），稅後
淨利 8.73 億元（+21%YoY），EPS 為 7.28 元。

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test

삼성전자  (005930) 
2026.11.04

STRESS TEST 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

목표주가 37만원, ‘28년 기준으로는 56만원까지 열림 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 37만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다. 이는 ‘00년 이후 25년간 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 
해당한다[표1]. ‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 56%, 59%, 48%로 지난 25년 평균인 
22%를 크게 상회할 전망이며, 고원형 ROE가 장기간 유지될 것으로 판단한다.  
이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해질 경우 시장의 시야는 ‘28년으로 향할 것이다. 
이 경우 ‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 56만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 43% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -41%를 넘어섰다. 특히 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
이러한 우려가 지속되는 한, 이익의 눈높이가 높아질수록 멀티플은 저렴해질 
뿐이며, DRAM 가격이 예상보다 더 올라갈 것이라는 소식이 주가의 촉매로 
작용하지 못한다. 결국 주가 반등을 위해서는 시장이 상정하고 있는 다운사이클이 
우려만큼 깊지 않을 것이라는 믿음이 필요한 것으로 본다.

STRESS TEST와 HBM 재평가 
① 당사는 고객들이 어느 정도 가격대에서 LTA를 해지하려는 유인이 생길지 
추정해봤다. Micron의 코멘트를 차용하여 ‘22~’23년 수준의 스트레스 테스트를 
시나리오 분석한 결과, 4Q27 고점 대비 DRAM 가격이 60% 수준 빠져야 해지가 
검토될 수 있다[그림2]. 그마저도 다음 공급 부족기의 물량 배정 우선순위 상실 
등 정성적 요인까지 감안하면 실제 해지 가능성은 더욱 낮은 것으로 판단한다. 
② HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 
중장기적으로 확대될 cHBM은 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 
재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 물량과 가격이 결정된다. 수요가 둔화되더라도 범용 
메모리처럼 재고 누적과 가격 급락으로 이어지기보다는 발주 조정이나 계약 
재협상을 통해 영향을 받는 구조다.  
동사 HBM 매출은 ‘26년 28조원에서 ‘28년 215조원으로 증가할 전망이다. 
‘27년 HBM 가격 방향성의 윤곽이 잡힌 현 시점에서, 동사 HBM 매출의 변수는 
가격이 아니라 물량이며, 물량은 고객과 사전에 협의되는 구조다. 과거 메모리 
업황에서는 2년 뒤의 이익을 가늠하기 어려웠으나, 이제는 HBM을 통해 중장기 
실적을 논의할 수 있게 됐다는 점, 그 자체에 주목한다. 
만약 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E (21배, 15배, 12배)를 
적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 19%, 69%, 108%를 설명한다[표3]. 
특히 cHBM이 본격화할 ‘28년에는 HBM만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명될 수 
있다는 의미다. 시장이 통상 받아들이는 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 다르지 
않다.

### Page 2

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
79

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간일 전망이다.  
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 업황 지속성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 이 불확실성이 
멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 
구간이 이어질 전망이다. 기회비용은 결국 환원수익률로 보상받아야 하므로, ‘27년 1월 신규 
주주환원 정책이 중요한 변곡점이 될 것으로 판단한다.  
당사는 동사가 ‘27~’29년 FCF의 50%를 주주에게 환원하고, 이 중 70%를 배당, 30%를 
자사주 매입/소각으로 쓰는 것으로 가정하여, 현재 주가 기준 ‘27년 15% 수준의 
주주환원수익률을 기대한다. 미국 10년물 국채 금리가 5% 안팎인 환경에서 무위험 금리의 
3배에 달하는 환원수익률, 그중 배당만으로 10% 수준의 수익률은 그 자체로 희소한 투자 
포인트로 볼 수 있다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는, 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 이후 신규 팹 물량이 
소량 출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 LTA가 이익을 지킨다는 사실이 
숫자로 확인되고 cHBM의 가치가 재평가된다면 순환 할인이 축소될 것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

## Document 273: AI股票抛售是否过度？还是华尔街抢先下调了预期？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI股票抛售是否过度？还是华尔街抢先下调了预期？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛数据显⽰，美国科技股多空⽐已降⾄ 2016 年以来最低， CTA 持有约 39 亿美元纳指空
头， AI 半导体板块 2028 年预期市盈率仅 11 倍，较标普 500 折价近三成，市场定价已隐含盈
利下滑约 6% 。但卖⽅仍预期板块 2028 年盈利增⻓ 31% 。这究竟意味着 AI 股票遭遇了过度
抛售，还是华尔街正在抢先下调盈利预期？  随着 AI 信⽤利差升⾄两年⾼位、融资压⼒持续
加剧，英伟达财报与 Anthropic IPO 将成为检验市场预期的关键。
摘要：
六周前，市场争论的还是 AI 仓位该持有多少；如今，问题已变成还有谁愿意接盘。AI 交易从⼈满
为患到⽆⼈问津，速度之快令⼈咋⾆，⽽这⼀切恰好发⽣在本年度 AI 主题最关键的⼀周前⼣。
据⾼盛资⾦流向策略师 Lee Coppersmith 在最新周报中指出，过去⼀个⽉，该⾏客⼾对 AI 主题的
情绪已 " 急剧转变 " ，悲观程度为近年来罕⻅，且这种悲观情绪已明显反映在仓位和估值层⾯。
与此同时，⾼盛旗下 AI 半导体篮⼦（ GSCBSMHX ）⽬前仅以 2028 年预期盈利的 11 倍交易，远

低于标普 500 的 16 倍 —— 尽管卖⽅共识预测该板块 2028 年盈利将增⻓ 31% 。
这⼀估值差异指向⼀个核⼼问题：要么此轮抛售已经过度，要么市场正在抢先定价盈利预期下
调。英伟达将于本周⼆发布财报（这也是创始⼈离任后的⾸份业绩）， Anthropic 的 IPO 则定于周
四登场，两⼤事件或将成为检验市场信⼼的关键节点。
从做多到做空：机构仓位急剧逆转
专业投资者的⾏动已远不⽌于减仓。 Coppersmith 援引⾼盛主经纪商数据显⽰，上周科技股再度
成为全球净卖出规模最⼤的板块，美国信息技术板块的多空⽐降⾄ 2016 年以来最低⽔平。更值
得关注的是，此前以平仓和降杠杆为主的资⾦流向，已转变为明显的新增做空⾏为。
与此同时，⻓线资⾦也在持续减持英伟达，转⽽流向超⼤规模云计算企业 —— ⽽后者的资本开
⽀，恰恰是英伟达的营收来源。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

系统性策略⽅⾯， CTA （商品交易顾问）同样在⼤举做空科技股。⾼盛模型显⽰，趋势跟踪基⾦
⽬前持有约 39 亿美元的纳斯达克 100 指数空头敞⼝，接近 2018 年底、 2022 年及 2025 年春季的阶
段性低点 —— ⽽上述每⼀次低点，事后均标志着⾏情的转折。
估值已隐含盈利萎缩，历史性折价引发争议
估值层⾯的数据同样引⼈注⽬。
⾃ 6 ⽉以来，费城半导体指数（ SOX ）相对标普 500 的跌幅已超过 30 个百分点，为 2002 年以来最
⼤相对回撤。截⾄上周五， SOX/SPX ⽐率较过去⼀年⾼点低 31.5% ，⽽历史最⼤跌幅为
57.3% ，出现于 2000 年 11 ⽉。
Coppersmith 指出，⾼盛研究团队认为部分个股的跌幅已经过度 —— 当前价格隐含的是 2028 年
盈利接近零增⻓的情景。若以与标普 500 相同的估值倍数衡量，当前股价实际上隐含该板块盈利
将下滑约 6% 。
历史规律表明，当卖⽅预期与市场定价出现如此剧烈的背离时，往往是盈利预期跟随股价下

调，⽽⾮股价向预期回归。价格⽬标同样倾向于跟随股价移动。
两⼤核⼼忧虑：谁来买单，能否建成
Coppersmith 将客⼾对话归结为两个核⼼问题：其⼀，谁来承担数千亿美元的 AI 追加⽀出？其
⼆，能否真正建成⾜够多的数据中⼼？
在数据中⼼建设⽅⾯，⾼盛研究团队认为市场担忧有所过度。即便在较为悲观的情景下， 2028
年新增数据中⼼容量仍将与 2027 年⼤体相当，若更多项⽬推进，规模甚⾄可能⾼出 50% 以上。
然⽽，据路透等媒体报道， Bernstein 对在建项⽬的独⽴审计显⽰，美国已公告数据中⼼容量
中，仅约四分之⼀具备可信度，这为乐观预期打上了折扣。
融资问题则被视为更根本的隐患。 Coppersmith 直⾔， AI 企业的⽀出已超过⾃⾝现⾦流，意味着
必须持续借债或引⼊外部投资者。然⽽借贷成本正在上升，投资者开始质疑巨额⽀出能否带来
回报。⾼盛投资研究部⻔认为，英伟达现有承诺或可维持，但同等规模的新融资将愈发困难。
信⽤市场的信号印证了这⼀判断：AI 相关投资级信⽤利差已扩⼤⾄约 140 个基点，为过去两年最

⾼⽔平，较⾮ AI 信⽤利差⾼出约 40 个基点。与此同时，⾼盛预计 2027 年美国信⽤债发⾏规模将
再创历史新⾼，超过 2.7 万亿美元。
英伟达财报与 Anthropic IPO ：催化剂还是顶部信号？

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
本周的两⼤事件或将为市场提供⽅向性参考。英伟达将于当地时间周⼆收盘后发布财报，该股
已连续六周下跌，累计跌幅达 30% 。
Anthropic 则定于周四上市，据报道认购订单已超额数倍，但⾄少有⼀位与 Peter Thiel 相关的投
资⼈警告，若上市表现疲软，可能引发整个 AI 交易的连锁崩塌。
即便两⼤事件均顺利过关， Coppersmith 援引⾼盛 2027 年市场展望指出， AI ⽀出增速可能于明年
初⻅顶，⽽市场对增速放缓的反应往往远⽐对绝对数字的反应更为敏感。
数据显⽰， AI 贡献了美国 GDP 增⻓的 21% ，却贡献了企业盈利增⻓的 62% ，以及标普 500 年内市
值涨幅峰值时的 92%—— 股市对 AI 的依赖程度远超实体经济。
仓位已净化，但融资才是真正考验
Coppersmith 的最终⽴场审慎⽽折中：科技股仓位已⼤幅净化，可逢弱势逐步加仓，但不宜追

涨，同时应保持对冲纪律，并关注 AI 基础设施以外的轮动机会。
仓位层⾯的极度净化 —— 科技股超配⽐例降⾄历史低位、 CTA 接近最⼤空头、半导体估值隐含盈
利萎缩 —— 确实为剧烈逼空⾏情提供了⼟壤，英伟达财报或 Anthropic 上市均可能成为导⽕索。
然⽽，仓位可以解释反弹，却⽆法为数据中⼼提供资⾦。真正的考验在信⽤市场： AI 利差处于两
年⾼位，⾼收益债买家已基本离场，⽽⾏业明年仍计划创纪录地举债融资。 Anthropic 的巨型
IPO 或许能短暂提振市场信⼼，但也可能成为最后⼀批⼤型 AI ⻛险买家悉数⼊场的时刻 —— 恰好
与⽀出增速⻅顶的时间窗⼝重合。
专业投资者已⽤空头仓位表明了⽴场。本周，市场将开始寻找答案。

Topics  Regulatory reform in India's insurance sector

Over the past quarter century, the Indian government has pursued the liberalization of the country's insurance market, 
with the aim of bringing stability to people's living standards and strengthening financial intermediation functions. 
Recently, however, it has begun to reverse course, tightening rules in order to better protect consumers.

■ India's insurance market has grown rapidly with deregulation 
The Indian government has long pursued 
the development of the country's insurance 
market, with the aim of bringing stability to 
people's living standards and strengthening 
financial intermediation functions. This 
stance can be attributed to two background 
factors:  
The first is that India's social security 
system remains underdeveloped. As a result, 
enrollment in health and life insurance can 
provide a vital safety net for mitigating the 
risk of sharp declines in household income 
due to unexpected illnesses or accidents.  
The second is that the sustainable 
development of the Indian economy requires 
the accumulation of long-term and stable 
domestic capital, separate from short-term 
bank deposits, in order to finance the 
development of infrastructure and the 
nurturing of industry. The hope has been that 
the enormous sums of money collected and 
managed by insurance companies can serve 
as a reliable source for this long-term 
financing.  
These factors have led the Indian 
government to walk a path of gradual 
deregulation over the past quarter century. 
The process began with the liberalization of 
market entry by private and foreign 
companies in 2000, which was followed by 
a lifting of the ban on "bancassurance" (the 
sale of insurance through bank branches) in 
2002, the raising of the ceiling on foreign 
ownership, and other reforms.  
This series of deregulatory measures 
spurred the entry of numerous private and foreign 
firms, causing market competition to intensify. Several 
Japanese insurers, including Nippon Life and the Tokio 
Marine Group, also entered the market, helping to drive 
investment in India.  
Besides deregulation related to market entry and 
sales, other factors also contributed to the rapid growth 
of India's insurance market, namely the expansion of 
the middle class, improvements in household financial 
literacy, and the digitalization of insurance services. 
Premium income grew at a pace exceeding economic 
growth, and in recent years insurance companies' assets 
under management have reached a level equivalent to 
Year Event
Establishment of the IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory
and Development Authority of India)
Acceptance of market entry applications from
domestic private firms and foreign entities
(foreign equity cap set at 26%)

through banks or bank-owned distribution
channels)

for the sale of new insurance products (replacing

incentives to sell products and inappropriate sales practices. This should shore up the credibility of the 
insurance market and the foundation for its sustainable development. 
On the minus side, there are worries about side effects arising from the transition. As rules on selling 
become stricter, insurance companies and the financial institutions acting as insurance agents will inevitably 
see some temporary moderation in the pace of their earnings growth. Moreover, given that insurance 
companies and financial institutions are major investors in India's financial markets, tighter regulation is 
likely to exert downward pressure on equity and bond prices. 
These changes in the insurance market provide an important perspective for assessing the future 
trajectory of the Indian economy. They illustrate that rapid market expansion driven by liberalization can 
create a variety of problems. And this dynamic is not limited to the insurance sector, as it can also occur 
across many other industries. At the same time, the redesign and recalibration of regulations in response to 
such developments can provide a foundation for a new stage of growth. The new insurance regulations 
could constitute an early example of this phenomenon, so will be closely watched to see what changes they 
bring in the years ahead.  
  (Shotaro Kumagai)

Topics  Talent travel curbs may weigh on China’s growth

In September, the Chinese government introduced new exit-entry rules. Indicative of an effort to retain talent in order 
to prevent technology leakage, the rules could narrow opportunities for China to absorb overseas knowledge and weigh 
on its economic growth over the medium to long term.

■ Scope of overseas travel restrictions expanded 
On September 15, the Chinese 
government brought in new rules 
specifying conditions and screening 
procedures for entry and exit. The new 
rules explicitly state that the Ministry of 
Commerce or other departments may 
prohibit Chinese citizens from leaving the 
country if they violate export-control or 
technology import/export regulations and 
are deemed likely to threaten national 
industrial or technological security.  
Under China's Exit and Entry 
Administration Law, which came into 
effect in 2013, defendants and suspects in 
criminal cases, as well as persons involved 
in unsettled civil cases whom courts have 
prohibited from leaving the country, had 
already been subject to exit restrictions. In 
addition, Chinese citizens could be denied 
permission to leave the country if the 
competent departments determined that 
their departure might endanger national 
security or interests. Aside from formal exit 
bans, personnel who handle state secrets 
and officials in the public sector have long 
been subject to controls such as centralized 
passport management and the need to 
obtain approval in advance of overseas travel. What the new rules effectively do is make it clear that exit 
can also be restricted when there are issues from the standpoint of economic security. 
Against this backdrop, it was reported at the end of September that overseas travel restrictions already 
imposed on key personnel in the AI and semiconductor sectors had been expanded to include their spouses, 
children, and other family members. According to these reports, these key personnel include prominent AI 
startup founders, senior executives at major private enterprises, and core R&D talent. Although the measure 
does not constitute a blanket travel ban, affected individuals and their families are reportedly required to 
obtain government approval for exit even in the case of short private trips or business travel. 
This regulatory tightening is thought to be related to the technological rivalry between the U.S. and China. 
As competition surrounding critical technologies such as generative AI intensifies, the Chinese government 
appears to view not only technologies themselves, but also the researchers and engineers who create them, 
as strategic assets. By widening travel curbs to encompass family members, the authorities are likely 
seeking to prevent overseas emigration by individuals or the relocation of R&D operations abroad. A 
noteworthy aspect of the recent reports is that the controls apparently now extend beyond the public sector 
to key personnel at private enterprises, as well as their families. Restrictions like these on talent mobility 
could undermine the mechanisms of technology absorption and productivity improvement that have 
hitherto facilitated China's growth.

### Page 6

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  November 2026, No.308

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

5 
■ China's productivity gains have been supported by market opening and technology 
absorption 
Productivity exerts a substantial influence on 
economic growth. Total factor productivity (TFP), an 
indicator of an economy's overall production 
efficiency, provides an estimate of efficiency in 
production that cannot be explained solely in terms of 
inputs of capital and labor, and reflects factors such as 
levels of technology and efficiency of resource 
allocation. 
China's TFP has been rising for many years. In 
addition to the transition to a market economy and the 
growth of private enterprises since the country adopted 
the policy of reform and opening up, Chinese 
productivity has benefitted from the absorption of 
foreign technology and managerial know‑how through 
trade and direct investment. A labor shift from 
agriculture to manufacturing and other sectors, along 
with competition among firms and the weeding out of 
weaker players, has also helped deliver productivity 
gains across the economy.  
Nevertheless, China's TFP remains low relative to 
other major economies. According to estimates from 
Penn World Table, China's TFP in 2023 was less than 
half that of the U.S., and it was also below the levels not 
only of Germany and other major European economies 
and of South Korea and Japan, but of Thailand and 
Malaysia, too. Factors behind this include inefficient 
resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced 
technologies and management practices. Preferential 
financial and institutional treatment for state‑owned 
enterprises, regional protectionism, and mechanisms 
that impede market entry and exit have distorted the 
allocation of resources, preventing capital and labor 
from being fully utilized by more productive firms and sectors. Moreover, although China has achieved 
strong competitiveness in certain advanced industries, such as electric vehicles and semiconductors, the 
leading-edge technologies and management practices developed in these sectors have not sufficiently 
diffused to the service sector and small and medium‑sized enterprises, which form the foundation of the 
economy, and this has constrained overall productivity growth.  
■ Moves to contain technology could hinder growth over the medium to long term 
Efforts to retain talent in order to prevent technology leakage could further delay China's productivity 
catch‑up with advanced economies. While restrictions on overseas travel by elite professionals may help 
with the retention of domestic R&D talent and protection of current technological advantages, they could 
also reduce opportunities to absorb overseas knowledge and to jointly develop new technologies with 
foreign partners. The transfer of technology and know‑how does not occur solely through the sharing of 
"explicit knowledge," in the form of academic papers, technical blueprints, and so on. "Tacit knowledge," 
which is transmitted through face‑to‑face discussion, collaborative work, and the acquisition of experience 
in production settings, is also crucial. If travel controls delay or prevent participation in international 
conferences, joint research projects, and technology exchanges with overseas companies, opportunities to 
absorb such knowledge may be lost, slowing technological innovation and improvements in production 
processes. Furthermore, if obtaining travel approval becomes more time‑consuming or less predictable, the 
overseas assignment or international deployment of technical personnel could also be obstructed. 
The new rules also cover the entry of foreign nationals. If uncertainty surrounding the administration of 
entry and exit controls increases, it could create obstacles to attracting and retaining highly skilled talent 
from abroad. At a time when inefficient resource allocation and the slow diffusion of advanced knowledge 
are already weighing on productivity growth in China, curbs on people-to- people exchange could further

impede both the absorption and creation of knowledge, as well as its practical application. Although the 
magnitude of the impact will depend on the scope of application and actual implementation on the ground, 
restrictions on talent exchanges and overseas travel that reflect excessive emphasis on technology 
protection could slow China's TFP growth over the medium to long term and act as a headwind to the 
Chinese economy by lowering its potential growth rate.

## Document 323: COP31_ Why it still matters for businesses

Source PDF: all report pdf/COP31_ Why it still matters for businesses.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Is COP still worth following? We think so. What matters for
businesses is whether this year’s COP31 can improve the
business case for action. Here are four areas to watch

Coco Zhang and Gerben Hieminga
The global climate landscape has changed signiﬁcantly since world
leaders committed 11 years ago to limiting global warming to well
below 2°C and pursuing eﬀorts to limit warming to 1.5°C.
Geopolitical tensions, policy shifts, trade protectionism, rising
energy demand… they have all made it harder for governments to
turn climate commitments into action.
That raises an important question for businesses: Is the Conference
of the Parties (COP) still worth following?
Our answer is yes.
Businesses do not just invest in climate targets; they invest in viable
projects and operations. But many of them still face economic and
practical barriers. Grid constraints and high electricity costs can
slow electriﬁcation. Greener industrial products can struggle to

### Page 2

attract buyers willing to pay a premium. And climate adaptation
plans do not always translate into investable projects.
Businesses cannot solve these challenges alone. In a 2025 UN
survey of more than 2,000 CEOs across 128 countries, 92% said
strong global governance and coordinated policy are “critical” or
“important” to implementing their sustainability agendas.
That is why COP is still relevant. It can help align policy priorities,
improving investment conditions, and connecting businesses with

the right resources. At COP31, four areas will be particularly
important for businesses to watch.
Electriﬁcation marries economic growth
with sustainability
Electriﬁcation is increasingly important for economic growth,
energy security, and sustainability. Demand from AI, EVs, heat
pumps and industrial decarbonisation is accelerating electricity
use, which is growing twice as fast as overall energy demand.
Electricity also punches above its weight economically, accounting

for 23% of global ﬁnal energy consumption but powering 46% of
global GDP. This implies that high value-added sectors rely more on
electricity than the overall economy.
Electricity’s economic contribution
outweighs its share of energy use

### Page 3

Source: ING Research, International Energy Agency
COP31’s push to accelerate electriﬁcation reﬂects this trend. The
COP31 presidency has set a goal of increasing electricity’s share of
ﬁnal energy consumption from 23% today to 35% by 2035.
Getting there will require addressing a major constraint: the grid. As
we have written, grid congestion could slow electriﬁcation, increase
costs, and delay electricity-dependent economic activity.
The key question at COP31 is how governments turn this ambition

into action. We think eﬀective action could include:
Establish a detailed electriﬁcation roadmap with interim
benchmarks to guide progress and sustain momentum across
future COPs
Enhance knowledge-sharing platforms. The International
Smart Grid Action Network has extensive experience sharing
best practices, but could go further by translating knowledge
into direct technical assistance and ready-to-deploy models
Set country- or sector-level targets and pathways to encourage

industry alignment and commitment
Improve access to ﬁnance by expanding dedicated ﬁnancing
for developing countries, while lowering upfront costs in Europe
Improve the economics. Coordinate energy pricing reforms to
make electriﬁcation more competitive with fossil fuel
combustion alternatives, including by reducing taxes and grid
fees that raise electricity prices
Strengthen infrastructure by encouraging governments to
reduce permitting barriers (e.g. Europe and the US), advance

### Page 4

regulatory support for power demand ﬂexibility, and expand
cross-border grids where needed (e.g. Southeast Asia)
If COP31 delivers meaningful action in these areas, the implications
for businesses could be signiﬁcant. Stronger policy could accelerate
near-term grid connections and enhance long-term investment
conﬁdence. Meanwhile, companies may need to work more closely
with utilities and regulators to secure the power they need.
Green industrialisation remains critical

But electriﬁcation is only part of the story. Industry is another
major source of process emissions, with sectors such as steel,
cement, and aviation accounting for roughly 20% of the global
total. Cutting these emissions is particularly diﬃcult because many
industrial processes still have few cost-eﬀective alternatives without
lengthy site overhauls.
COP31 has therefore put green industrialisation high on the
agenda, building on COP30’s progress. Following the Belém

Declaration on Global Green Industrialisation at COP30, a coalition
led by the Brazilian and UK governments mobilised hundreds of
millions of dollars in concessional ﬁnance for Brazilian industries
and awarded funding to 12 hydrogen projects. Over time, these
eﬀorts could reshape manufacturing processes and investment.
But many industrial decarbonisation technologies still face
signiﬁcant barriers to scaling. Their high cost premiums mean
economics depend heavily on government support. Demand for

cleaner industrial products remains limited. And the necessary
infrastructure for many is still underdeveloped.
Hard-to-abate sectors’ green premium is
way higher than what companies are willing
to pay

Source: World Economic Forum, ING Research
COP31 is not setting a new level of ambition for green
industrialisation. It’s focusing on implementing the existing
agenda. One priority is narrowing the demand gap. COP31 can
coordinate governments to create more demand for low-carbon
industrial products through long-term public procurement. If such
eﬀorts gain momentum at COP31, they could provide the long-run
revenue certainty investors need to back green industrial projects at

scale.
Climate ﬁnance faces multiple gaps
To realise the targets and priorities set forth at COP, investment is
crucial. And total ﬂows are growing. Global climate ﬁnance reached
around $2tn in 2024 and continued to grow in 2025, although at a
slower rate.

But the headline number masks signiﬁcant gaps. First, even though
climate mitigation (i.e. emissions reduction) ﬁnance accounted for
$1.9tn of the $2tn total, the level falls well short of estimated
needs, which range from $7.6tn-$9.6tn through 2050. In areas such
as electriﬁcation and green industrialisation, funding is needed not
only for technologies, but also for the infrastructure and demand

needed to scale them.
Second, only $64bn of the total went exclusively to adaptation, an
area requiring surging investment because of intensifying climate
risks. Third, a substantial share of climate ﬁnance stays within
advanced economies or comes from domestic sources.
International ﬂows to help developing countries totalled only
$175bn, far below COP29’s target of $1.3tn per year by 2035.

More climate products and solutions are emerging in the latter two
segments. But their higher risks or weaker business cases can make
it diﬃcult to attract capital without supportive policies. Investors are
actively turning to blended ﬁnance, which uses public capital to
help de-risk projects and mobilise private investment. Yet many still
lack the data, resources and connections needed to move projects

forward. This is where COP31 can help. As we discuss in the next
section, it can help governments create more favourable
investment conditions and connect suppliers, buyers and investors.
Adaptation is becoming a business
imperative and opportunity
Business awareness of climate adaptation has grown, as many
businesses are experiencing more disruptions ﬁrsthand. Our
previous analysis also shows how drought on Europe’s Rhine River,
hurricanes in the US, and other extreme weather events are already

aﬀecting supply chains across sectors. In aggregate, physical
climate risks could cost companies in the S&P Global 1200 index
$1.2tn annually by 2050.
One barrier to corporate investment in climate adaptation is that it
is still seen primarily as a way to avoid losses. But adaptation can
also create business value. Investments in resilient assets, cooling
systems, supplier diversiﬁcation and climate-resilient materials can
improve reliability, competitiveness and long-term growth.

Research from the World Resources Institute suggests that every $1
invested in adaptation can generate up to $10 in beneﬁts. That
return could be even higher for highly climate-exposed sectors. For
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 8

example, the World Economic Forum estimates returns of $19–$43
per dollar invested in the food and beverage sector.
COP can help turn adaptation priorities into investable projects.
Since the Paris Agreement, countries have developed National
Adaptation Plans (NAPs), but many remain high-level and lack
speciﬁc, actionable projects. Recent COP eﬀorts have sought to
change this by encouraging governments to translate their NAPs
into portfolios of investable adaptation projects.

But identifying projects does not guarantee ﬁnancing. This is where
COP31 could make a diﬀerence. Its proposed Climate
Implementation Bridge (BRIDGE) initiative could make projects
easier for investors to identify and assess by centralising
information on technical requirements, risks, costs and beneﬁts.
Expanding the initiative to more countries could give more banks,
investors and companies access to potential adaptation
investments.
For businesses, a successful COP31 could therefore help unlock

capital for adaptation projects. The beneﬁts could extend
throughout supply chains, as companies increasingly depend on
the climate resilience of their suppliers to maintain reliable
operations.
Conclusion
The most eﬀective corporate sustainability strategies support long-
term growth, but they are more eﬀective with international
coordination and strong policy support. COP can bring
governments and businesses together to provide such a
framework, even amid geopolitical tensions and concerns over

slowing progress. COP31 may not deliver a landmark global
agreement, but practical advances in these four priorities could still
have immediate relevance for businesses.

## Document 344: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-16).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 383: Morning Report (2026-11-17)

Risk sentiment softened 
on Friday as renewed tech 
selling weighed on equities, 
although a softer-than-
expected US producer price 
report tempered expectations 
for aggressive Fed tightening. 
Despite Friday’s weakness, 
the S&P 500 and Nasdaq still 
gained over the week. 
Global bond markets were 
mixed. US 2-year Treasury 
yields fell 4bps following 
the softer prices print, while 
longer-term yields generally 
moved higher across Europe 
and Japan. 
Australian yields pushed 
higher and the Australian 
dollar weakened. The 3-year 
and 10-year government 
bond yields rose 7bps and 
8bps respectively, while the 
Australian dollar fell 0.5% 
to US$0.6772. Attention 
now turns to this week’s 
labour force survey and its 
implications for RBA pricing. 
Commodity markets were 
generally weaker, with oil, 
copper, coking coal and iron 
ore all declining. Oil ended 
the week almost 3.0% lower.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• Risk sentiment softened into the end of the week as
renewed selling in tech stocks weighed on equities. The
moves followed last week’s turmoil at Nvidia, although a
softer-than-expected US producer price report provided

some offset by tempering expectations for aggressive Fed
rate hikes.
• Key US equity indices finished Friday mostly lower, led by
the tech sector. The S&P 500 declined 0.4% to 7,562 but
finished the week 2.5% higher. The tech-heavy Nasdaq
underperformed, falling 0.9% on the day, although it ended
the week 3.5% higher. The Dow Jones Industrial Average
gained 0.1% and finished the week around 2.0% higher.
• European equities were mixed on Friday but ended the week

firmly in the green, underpinned by lower oil prices earlier
in the week and solid earnings from heavyweights. The
Euro Stoxx 50 fell 0.2% on Friday but ended the week 3.5%
higher, while Germany’s DAX was unchanged on the day
but 3.6% higher over the week. The FTSE 100 rose 0.4% on
the day and finished the week 2.1% higher. Asian markets
were mixed, with the Nikkei rising 0.5% and the Hang Seng
losing 1.6%. Locally, the S&P/ASX 200 fell 0.7% to 9,082.

Equity futures point to a broadly flat open, with the SPI 200
unchanged at 9,114.
• Outside the US, bond yields generally moved higher. In the
US, bonds rallied on the back of the softer-than-expected
producer price index and some dovish Fed speak. The yield
on the policy-sensitive two-year US Treasury fell 4bps to
4.84%, while the 10-year Treasury yield increased 1bp to
5.14%. Traders are now pricing around 25bps of rate hikes
by the end of 2026 and almost 60bps by the end of 2027.

Germany’s 10-year Bund yield rose 3bps to 3.38%, UK
10-year gilt yields increased 4bps to 5.34%, and Japanese
10-year government bond yields rose 6bps to 2.87%.
• Australian bond yields moved higher. The 3-year
government bond yield rose 7bps to 4.79%, while the
10-year yield increased 8bps to 5.12%. In futures trading,
the 3-year government futures yield rose a further 1bp to
4.79%, while the 10-year futures yield increased 2bps to
5.15%. OIS markets expect the RBA Board to remain on

hold through much of 2027. Traders are pricing almost no
chance of a December hike and around 7bps of tightening
at the February 2027 meeting, before pricing shifts towards
the possibility of rate cuts later in 2027. The labour force
survey later this week will be closely watched and is likely
to influence market pricing.
• In foreign exchange markets, the US dollar strengthened
modestly, with the DXY rising 0.2% to 103.41. The
Australian dollar fell 0.5% against the US dollar to

US$0.6772, while the trade-weighted index declined 0.5%
to 63.3. Against the major crosses, the Australian dollar fell
0.6% against the yen, 0.4% against sterling, 0.1% against
the New Zealand dollar, 0.2% against the euro, and 0.4%
against both the Canadian and Singapore dollars.
• Commodity markets were generally weaker. The CRB Index
fell 0.5%, while WTI crude oil declined 0.2% to US$88.56
a barrel. Gold edged 0.2% higher to US$3,884.49/oz, while

copper fell 1.4%. Coking coal declined 0.4% to US$252.00/t
and iron ore fell 0.5% to US$90.00/t. Thermal coal bucked
the broader trend, rising 0.3% to US$145.75/t, while ACCU
prices fell 4.3% to $36.13.
International Data
US PPI slowed in October from 1.4% to 0.3%. Excluding food
and energy, momentum softened from 0.8% to 0.3%.
New York and Chicago Fed Presidents Williams and Goolsbee
kept the focus on inflation pressures and upside price risks
on Friday, and remained of the view that further tightening is

necessary. As for other recent Fed speakers, the labour market
is not currently a concern.
Euro Area GDP’s second estimate for Q3 met expectations,
unrevised from the flash estimate at 0.4%, 1.3%yr.
The Euro Area trade deficit widened at the margin in
September from a revised EUR7.8bn to EUR8.7bn.
Local Data
Westpac Economics released its November Market Outlook,
with updated domestic and global forecasts (see here).
We reaffirm our view that the RBA’s tightening cycle is over,

with our Chief Economist highlighting greater conviction in the
call (see here).
